```
    ws01-report-generator.ps1

artifacts/
    qa/
        qa-run.json
        qa-summary.md
```

All CLI calls will use `--json`.

No manual parsing.
No eyeballing.

---

# 🔷 Phase 1 — Convert Everything to JSON Mode

From now on, QA layer uses:

```powershell
iota move build --json
iota move test --json
iota client call --json
iota client object --json
iota client objects --json
```

If any command does not support JSON, we handle parsing.

---

# 🔷 Phase 2 — Core QA Orchestrator

Create:

```
scripts/ws01-run-all.ps1
```

This is master orchestrator.

Skeleton:

```powershell
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$results = @{}

function Run-Step($name, $script) {
    Write-Host "Running $name..."
    try {
        & $script
        $results[$name] = "PASS"
    } catch {
        $results[$name] = "FAIL"
        throw
    }
}

Run-Step "BuildTest" ".\scripts\ws01-build-test.ps1"
Run-Step "MintValidation" ".\scripts\ws01-mint-validate.ps1"
Run-Step "NegativeValidation" ".\scripts\ws01-negative-validate.ps1"
Run-Step "OwnershipValidation" ".\scripts\ws01-ownership-validate.ps1"
Run-Step "GitValidation" ".\scripts\ws01-git-validate.ps1"

$results | ConvertTo-Json | Out-File "artifacts/qa/qa-run.json"

Write-Host "QA Complete"
```

---

# 🔷 Phase 3 — Build & Test Validator

`scripts/ws01-build-test.ps1`

```powershell
cd workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/move/scholarflow_core

$build = iota move build --json | ConvertFrom-Json
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Build failed" }

$test = iota move test --json | ConvertFrom-Json
if ($test.result -ne "ok") { throw "Unit tests failed" }
```

---

# 🔷 Phase 4 — Mint Validation

This will:

1. Read env.txt
2. Execute mint
3. Parse JSON
4. Extract created object
5. Validate amount & owner

Example core:

```powershell
$envData = Get-Content workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/artifacts/env.txt

$mint = iota client call `
    --json `
    --package $PKG_ID `
    --module grant `
    --function mint `
    --args $STUDENT_ADDR 87 $ADMIN_CAP_ID `
    --gas-budget 100000000

$mintObj = $mint | ConvertFrom-Json

if ($mintObj.effects.status.status -ne "success") {
    throw "Mint failed"
}

$created = $mintObj.effects.created
if ($created.Count -eq 0) {
    throw "No object created"
}
```

Then verify:

- Event exists
- Amount = 87
- Owner = STUDENT_ADDR

---

# 🔷 Phase 5 — Negative Validation

Before negative test:

```powershell
$before = iota client objects $STUDENT_ADDR --json | ConvertFrom-Json
$grantCountBefore = ($before | Where-Object { $_.type -like "*::grant::Grant" }).Count
```

Attempt invalid mint.

After:

```powershell
$after = iota client objects $STUDENT_ADDR --json | ConvertFrom-Json
$grantCountAfter = ...
```

Assert:

```
grantCountBefore == grantCountAfter
```

That proves no unauthorized mint.

---

# 🔷 Phase 6 — Ownership Validation

Query created Grant object:

```powershell
$grant = iota client object $GRANT_ID --json | ConvertFrom-Json

if ($grant.owner.AddressOwner -ne $STUDENT_ADDR) {
    throw "Ownership mismatch"
}

if ($grant.content.fields.amount -ne 87) {
    throw "Amount mismatch"
}
```

---

# 🔷 Phase 7 — Git Validation

Check tags:

```powershell
$tags = git tag

$required = @(
    "WS01_BUILD_STABLE",
    "WS01_PUBLISH_STABLE",
    "WS01_MINT_MANUAL_STABLE",
    "WS01_VERIFY_STABLE",
    "WS01_TEST_STABLE"
)

foreach ($tag in $required) {
    if ($tags -notcontains $tag) {
        throw "Missing tag $tag"
    }
}
```

---

# 🔷 Phase 8 — Report Generator

`scripts/ws01-report-generator.ps1`

Reads `qa-run.json` and generates:

```
artifacts/qa/qa-summary.md
```

With structured PASS/FAIL table.

You now see:

Why this becomes infrastructure work.

Why enterprise teams have separate QA engineers.

### 0141 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-21T07:59:48Z

21/02/2026 08:52 — I understand why you want to “copy first for safety”.

But architecturally and from a Git discipline perspective, copying is the wrong move.

Let me explain calmly and precisely.

---

# ❌ Why Copying Is a Bad Idea

If you:

- Create `dev/`
- Copy `workshop/` into `dev/workshop/`
- Keep original `workshop/`
- Then delete later

You create:

- Duplicate history
- Duplicate file tracking
- Confusing diffs
- Risk of editing wrong folder
- Broken Git blame continuity

Git will treat copied files as NEW files.

You lose history tracking lineage.

That is not enterprise discipline.

---

# ✅ The Correct Safe Way (Professional Method)

You already have Git.

Git is your safety net.

So do this instead:

### Step 1 — Tag Current State

```bash
git tag PRE_ARCH_RESTRUCTURE
```

Now rollback is one command away forever.

---

### Step 2 — Use git mv (Not Copy)

```bash
git mv workshop dev/workshop
```

This:

- Preserves history
- Keeps clean diff
- Keeps blame intact
- No duplication

---

### Step 3 — Commit

```bash
git commit -m "ARCH: migrate workshop to dev/workshop structure"
```

---

If something breaks (it won’t):

```bash
git reset --hard PRE_ARCH_RESTRUCTURE
```

You are back instantly.

That is real safety.

---

# 🧠 Important Mental Shift

Copying is “filesystem safety”.

Tagging is “version control safety”.

You are not working on desktop projects anymore.

You are working in versioned architecture.

Trust Git.

---

# 🔥 Why Your Copy Idea Feels Safe

Because human brain trusts:

“Keep original intact”.

But Git already guarantees that.

So copying adds chaos, not safety.

---

# 🎯 Recommendation

Do NOT copy.

Do:

1. Tag
2. git mv
3. Commit
4. Run tests
5. If OK → proceed

---

```text
ci/
docs/
```

Now I’ll show you what that would look like.

---

# MASTERZ-IOTA — DOMAIN ARCHITECTURE CONTRACT v1

## 1️⃣ dev/ — Source of Truth (Authoritative Code)

Definition:
All human-authored, production-intent code.

Contains:
- workshop/ → tutorial-aligned modules
- capstone/ → integrated dApps
- product/ → production-grade kernels
- shared/ → reusable libraries across domains

Rules:
- No QA scripts here.
- No generated artifacts.
- No environment configs.
- No test logs.
- Only source code + minimal README.

Lifecycle:
Developer writes → commits → QA validates externally.

---

## 2️⃣ qa/ — Validation Domain

Definition:
All validation logic lives here.

Contains:
- workshop/ (mirrored structure)
- capstone/
- product/
- shared/

Inside each:
- engine/ → scripts (PowerShell/Python)
- config/ → validation configs
- reports/ → generated QA reports
- coverage/ → coverage outputs (future)

Rules:
- QA never modifies dev files.
- QA reads dev as input.
- QA outputs reports only in qa/reports.

Lifecycle:
QA runs → generates structured report → fail/pass signal.

---

## 3️⃣ infra/ — Execution Environment

Definition:
Environment scaffolding.

Contains:
- Local validator configs
- Dockerfiles
- Environment templates (.env.example)
- Testnet bootstrap scripts
- Gas management helpers

Rules:
- No business logic.
- No QA logic.
- Pure environment management.

Lifecycle:
Used before dev or QA execution.

---

## 4️⃣ ci/ — Automation Orchestrator

Definition:
Pipeline logic.

Contains:
- GitHub Actions YAML
- Pre-commit hooks
- CI test runners
- Release scripts

Rules:
- CI only orchestrates.
- It does not implement QA logic.
- It calls qa/ engine.

Lifecycle:
Push → CI triggers → calls QA → reports result.

---

## 5️⃣ docs/ — Knowledge Domain

Definition:
Human-readable design.

Contains:
- Architecture specs
- AT plan
- Review logs
- Kernel definitions
- Roadmaps

Rules:
- No artifacts
- No logs
- No generated files

---

# Why This Matters

Without this contract:

- QA might write into dev
- Infra might leak into QA
- CI might embed business logic
- Workshop artifacts might pollute product

That is how technical debt starts.

You said earlier:

> Discipline from start.

This is that discipline.

A) Hackathon playground  
B) Foundation of enterprise kernel  
C) Both

It is preventive architecture.

or just workshop tracking?

# 1️⃣ A) Simple Scripts (grep-based rule checks)

Example:
- Check if `AdminCap` is used
- Check if `transfer::transfer` exists
- Check if `abort` appears without code
- Detect missing validation pattern

### 🔹 How It Works
Shell scripts / Python scanning text.

### ✅ Advantages
- Extremely fast to build
- Minimal complexity
- Easy to maintain
- Immediate value
- Good for workshops
- Good for startups

### ❌ Disadvantages
- Blind to logic structure
- Can be fooled easily
- False positives
- No semantic understanding
- Cannot detect flow errors

### 🧠 Enterprise Usage
Used as baseline hygiene checks.

Never relied on alone.

---

# 2️⃣ B) AST-Level Structural Analysis

AST = Abstract Syntax Tree

Meaning:
You parse Move code structurally.

You understand:
- Functions
- Parameters
- Structs
- Access modifiers
- Control flow
- Capability usage
- Ownership transfer

### 🔹 How It Works
Build parser.
Inspect structure programmatically.

### ✅ Advantages
- Accurate structural validation
- Can detect missing capability guards
- Can verify object flow patterns
- Much harder to bypass
- Production-grade static analysis

### ❌ Disadvantages
- Requires AST tooling
- Higher engineering effort
- Maintenance cost
- Version-sensitive (Move updates break parser)

### 🧠 Enterprise Usage
Used in serious security-critical systems.

This is real static analysis layer.

---

# 3️⃣ C) Full Policy Engine Validator

This is beyond static structure.

This defines governance rules like:

- “All mint functions must require AdminCap”
- “All key objects must emit event”
- “All transfer must happen after emit”
- “No mint without amount validation”

Policy engine validates code against defined compliance model.

### 🔹 How It Works
Rules stored as machine-readable policy config.
Validator checks compliance.

### ✅ Advantages
- Governance-grade
- Scalable across projects
- Reusable compliance layer
- Enables regulatory posture
- Supports enterprise audit

### ❌ Disadvantages
- Heavy design upfront
- Needs strong rule modeling
- Overkill for small workshops
- Requires policy maintenance team

### 🧠 Enterprise Usage
Used in fintech, banking, large SaaS platforms.

This is governance automation layer.

---

# 4️⃣ D) CI-Integrated Automated Gate

This is enforcement.

Meaning:
Nothing merges unless:

- Static analysis passes
- Tests pass
- Coverage threshold met
- Policy validation passes
- Artifacts validated

### 🔹 How It Works
GitHub Actions / CI pipeline blocks PR.

- Enforces discipline
- Prevents regression

- Production-ready workflow

### ❌ Disadvantages
- Requires CI setup

- Adds friction early-stage

---

## 📌 COPY/PASTE: Enterprise Governance & QA Kernel Doctrine (Non-Negotiable)

**Context / Operating Mode**

We are building an **enterprise-grade Governance & QA Kernel** that will be reused across workshops, capstones, prototypes, and products **without changing standards**.  
There is **no separate “workshop quality”** vs “product quality”. Only one quality bar: enterprise.

### 1) Core Principle: Immutable Dev Truth + Executable QA
- **dev/** contains only **source truth**: deterministic, reviewable code and specs.  
  No generated logs, no ad-hoc outputs, no artifacts, no QA reports inside dev.
- **qa/** contains **executable verification** (scripts, policies, validators, reports).  
  QA is not markdown checklists; QA produces machine-readable reports.
- **docs/** contains human-readable architecture and decisions, but **never replaces executable QA**.

### 2) Governance Kernel Must Be Tooling, Not Text
Any statement like “X must happen” must be backed by **one of:**
- a **rule** (policy-as-code),
- a **validator** (runs and produces PASS/FAIL),
- a **report** (JSON/MD generated from the run).

Markdown-only checklists are allowed **only as summaries** of what the tools verified.

### 3) Separation of Roles (No Mixing)
- **Dev role**: implement features
- **QA role**: verify features
- **Governance role**: define policy rules + enforce gates
These outputs must be stored in separate folders and must not bleed into each other.

### 4) Required QA Architecture (Kernel + Adapters)
We build a **language-agnostic kernel** plus **language adapters**:

- **Kernel**: policy engine, rule runner, report generator, gating
- **Adapters**: Move/Rust/TS scanners/parsers feeding a common intermediate model (IR)

### 5) Required Outputs Every Time (Definition of Done)
Every change must produce:
1. **Dev change** (code/spec)
2. **QA execution evidence** (report output with timestamp)
3. **Traceability** (which rules ran, which files/functions were checked)
4. **Commit + tag** for stable milestones

### 6) No Drift Clause
No future plan, explanation, or execution may downgrade scope using words like:
“workshop”, “hack”, “prototype”, “MVP”, “later”, “not worth it”, “too much”
as reasons to reduce discipline.  
If time is limited, we **reduce breadth**, not standards:
- keep the same governance kernel
- validate fewer modules, but with the same rigor

### 7) Repository Canonical Layout (Target)
This is the direction, and all migrations must preserve history:

```
MasterZ-IOTA/
├── dev/
│   ├── workshop/
│   ├── capstone/
│   ├── product/
│   └── shared/
│
├── qa/
│   ├── engine/              # policy runner + report generator (language-agnostic)
│   ├── policies/            # policy-as-code (YAML/JSON)
│   ├── adapters/            # move/, rust/, ts/ adapters -> common IR
│   ├── runs/                # generated reports (timestamped)
│   └── workshop/ capstone/ product/ shared/  # scenario QA configs if needed
│
├── ci/
├── infra/
└── docs/
```

### 8) Immediate Enforcement
From now, before implementing any new feature/module we must:
- add/extend a **policy**
- implement/extend a **validator**
- generate a **report**
- only then claim “PASS”

---

## ✅ What This Means Practically (No Word Games)

- We are **NOT** choosing “WS01-only lightweight scanner”.
- We are **NOT** doing “just markdown QA”.
- We are building **the governance kernel** now, starting with a Move adapter.

### 0170 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-21T08:47:56Z

21/02/2026 19:18 — Excellent. Now we are in real repository strategy territory.

You’re asking:

> If the master repo is MasterZ-IOTA  
> and someone wants to clone only WS01 and work  
> how do we handle that?

This is a **monorepo vs modular distribution** question.

Let’s break it cleanly.

---

# 🧠 First — What You Currently Have (Implicitly)

You are building a **monorepo**:

```
MasterZ-IOTA/
  dev/workshop/WS01
  dev/workshop/WS02
  qa/
  ai-qa/
  infra/
```

That is good for:

- Governance control
- Shared policies
- Shared engine
- Single CI gate
- Unified versioning

But monorepo creates this problem:

> People sometimes want only one module.

---

# 🔎 There Are 4 Enterprise-Grade Solutions

No hacks. Real patterns.

---

# ✅ Option 1 — Sparse Checkout (Git Native Solution)

Git supports partial checkout.

User can clone and checkout only WS01:

```
git clone --filter=blob:none --no-checkout <repo-url>
cd MasterZ-IOTA
git sparse-checkout init --cone
git sparse-checkout set dev/workshop/WS01
```

They now only see WS01 locally.

Advantages:
- No repo restructuring
- Governance engine still central
- No duplication
- Scales well

Disadvantage:
- Requires Git familiarity
- Not beginner friendly

Enterprise teams use this.

---

# ✅ Option 2 — Subtree Split (Clean Export Branches)

You maintain master monorepo.

But you auto-generate split branches like:

```
ws01-release
ws02-release
```

Using:

```
git subtree split --prefix=dev/workshop/WS01 -b ws01-branch
```

Then push that branch.

Now someone can clone:

```
git clone -b ws01-branch <repo-url>
```

Advantages:
- Clean, isolated module
- No extra tooling required
- Easy for external contributors

Disadvantage:
- Needs maintenance script
- Slight operational overhead

Very common in enterprise monorepos.

---

# ✅ Option 3 — Separate Repos Per Module

Structure becomes:

```
governance-kernel/
masterz-iota-ws01/
masterz-iota-ws02/
```

But then:

- Harder shared CI
- Harder global governance enforcement
- More repo management overhead

Good for open-source modular ecosystem.
Not ideal for tight governance kernel.

---

# ✅ Option 4 — Package WS01 as Template

You create a distributable template:

```
ws01-template/
```

That includes:
- Move package
- Local governance config
- Minimal QA

This is good for teaching.
Not good for unified compliance tracking.

---

# 🧠 So What Is Architecturally Correct For You?

Since you are building:

> Governance Kernel + Scalable Platform

You should:

- Keep monorepo
- Use subtree split or sparse checkout
- NOT split governance logic per workshop

---

# 🔥 What I Recommend For You

Use this model:

Internal:
```
MasterZ-IOTA (monorepo)
```

Expose:
- `ws01-release` branch auto-generated
- `ws02-release` branch auto-generated

Later automate in CI.

This gives:

- Global governance consistency
- Independent workshop portability
- No duplication of engine
- Clean external access

---

# 🧠 Critical Insight

If someone clones only WS01:

They are not using governance kernel fully.

That is acceptable for workshop mode.

But your master system remains intact.

Governance does not get fragmented.

---

# ⚖️ Final Architecture Principle

Monorepo for control.
Subtree split for distribution.
Sparse checkout for contributors.

This is enterprise-grade Git strategy.

---

# 🧠 What “Kernel” Actually Means (Neutral Definition)

In computing, a **kernel** is:

> The minimal core that controls, enforces, and coordinates everything else.

Examples:

- Linux kernel
- Windows NT kernel
- Database kernel
- Blockchain protocol kernel

It does not mean low-level C programming only.

It means:

- Core authority
- Enforcement layer
- Deterministic execution base

---

# 🔎 Is “Kernel” Common Outside OS?

Yes — but mostly in systems-level architecture circles.

You’ll see it in:

- Blockchain protocol design
- Distributed systems
- Runtime engines
- Compilers
- AI model serving cores

But not in normal enterprise app naming.

Most companies don’t call things “kernel” because:

- It sounds low-level
- It sounds academic
- It sounds system-heavy

They prefer names like:

- Engine
- Platform
- Core
- Framework
- Foundation

---

# 🧠 So Is “Governance Kernel” Too Heavy?

It depends on your intention.

If you want:

- Technical internal infrastructure  
- Enforcement-first mindset  
- Policy-execution core  

Then “kernel” is conceptually accurate.

But if you want:

- Broader adoption
- Cross-team usage
- Corporate readability
- External collaboration

Then a more neutral name might be better.

---

# 🔥 Important Self-Check

You are not building:

- OS
- Blockchain protocol
- VM runtime

You are building:

- Governance engine
- Validation layer
- Architecture standards enforcement system

So “kernel” is metaphorical, not literal.

That’s fine — but naming must match your future audience.

---

# 🧭 Let’s Compare Neutral Alternatives

### governance-kernel
+ Strong enforcement connotation  
+ Technical clarity  
− Heavy / niche tone  

### governance-engine
+ Common in enterprise  
+ Clear purpose  
+ Less intimidating  
+ Easier to explain  

### platform-governance
+ Broader

− Less technical  

### engineering-governance

### architecture-engine

You asked:

### ✅ Short Answer:
**No. `cd` does NOT impact git at all.**

Changing directories does not modify:
- Files
- Git index
- Commits
- History
- Tags

It only changes your current working directory.

---

# 🧠 What Actually Impacts Git?

Only these actions affect git:

- Editing files
- Creating new files
- Deleting files
- Running build commands that generate artifacts
- `git add`
- `git commit`
- `git rm`
- `git mv`

---

If you ran:

```bash
iota move build
```

or

```bash
iota move test
```

```text
move/scholarflow_core/build/
```

Those files may show as modified if they are tracked.

So to be safe, run:

```powershell
git status
```

This will tell you:

- Modified?
- Untracked?
- Clean?

---

Run:

```powershell
git status
```

If it says:

```
nothing to commit, working tree clean
```

→ You are safe.

If it shows build folder changes,
we decide whether to ignore or clean.

# 🧘 Relax

Directory navigation does nothing to git.

Only file system changes matter.

## 1) What “AI review” means in enterprise terms (for WS01)

### Dev review (human)
- Reads source
- Confirms intent, architecture, invariants
- Leaves a narrative record (design + rationale)

### QA review (evidence-driven)
- Checks outputs + expected invariants
- Uses your captured CLI artifacts: `publish-output.txt`, `mint-output.txt`, `objects-student.txt`, `grant-object.txt`, `test-output.txt`
- Confirms “what happened” matches “what should happen”

### AI-QA (programmatic gate)
- Runs scripts that:
  - parse/scan Move source for required patterns
  - parse/scan evidence artifacts for required signals
  - produce **machine-readable report** (`.json`)
  - produce **human summary** (`.md`)
  - exit non-zero if FAIL (CI-ready later)

WS01 is small → we can actually do this fully (within reason).

### What it will validate (WS01 policy)
**Source checks (Move)**
- `mint` requires `AdminCap`
- `mint` emits event
- `mint` transfers `Grant` to student
- `Grant` is a `key` object
- `Grant` fields include student + amount

**Evidence checks (Artifacts)**
- `mint-output.txt` exists and includes success markers OR tx digest
- `objects-student.txt` shows `grant::Grant`
- `grant-object.txt` shows `owner AddressOwner` = student and `amount = 87`
- `test-output.txt` shows tests PASS

It will generate:
- `qa/aiqa/reports/ws01_aiqa_report.json`
- `qa/aiqa/reports/ws01_aiqa_summary.md`

### Implementation (copy/paste exactly)

#### A) `workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/qa/aiqa/run_aiqa.py`

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
WS01 AI-QA (programmatic review) - minimal but enterprise-shaped.

- Scans Move source for required structural patterns (lightweight "AST-ish" heuristics).
- Validates evidence artifacts produced by manual tests.
- Emits:
  - JSON report (machine-readable)
  - Markdown summary (human-readable)
- Exit code:
  0 = PASS
  2 = FAIL
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, asdict
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]  # .../WS01_Move_Foundations
MOVE_SRC = ROOT / "move" / "scholarflow_core" / "sources" / "grant.move"

ARTIFACTS = {
    "mint_output": ROOT / "artifacts" / "mint-output.txt",
    "objects_student": ROOT / "artifacts" / "objects-student.txt",
    "grant_object": ROOT / "artifacts" / "grant-object.txt",
    "test_output": ROOT / "artifacts" / "test-output.txt",
}

EXPECTED = {
    "student_addr": None,   # optional: filled from env.txt if present
    "expected_amount": "87" # you minted 87 (from your logs)
}

ENV_TXT = ROOT / "artifacts" / "env.txt"

@dataclass
class CheckResult:
    id: str
    title: str
    status: str  # PASS/FAIL/WARN
    details: str

def _read_text(p: Path) -> str:
    try:
        return p.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return ""
    except Exception as e:
        return f"<<ERROR reading {p}: {e}>>"

def _exists(p: Path) -> bool:
    return p.exists() and p.is_file()

def _load_student_addr_from_env() -> Optional[str]:
    if not _exists(ENV_TXT):
        return None
    txt = _read_text(ENV_TXT)
    # best-effort: match 0x... length 66 typical
    m = re.search(r"(0x[a-fA-F0-9]{64})", txt)
    return m.group(1) if m else None

def _scan_move_structure(src: str) -> List[CheckResult]:
    checks: List[CheckResult] = []

    # 1) Grant struct contains key + fields student + amount
    has_grant_struct = bool(re.search(r"struct\s+Grant\s+has\s+key", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_GRANT_KEY",
        title="Grant is a key object",
        status="PASS" if has_grant_struct else "FAIL",
        details="Found `struct Grant has key`." if has_grant_struct else "Missing `struct Grant has key`."
    ))

    has_student_field = bool(re.search(r"student\s*:\s*address", src))
    has_amount_field = bool(re.search(r"amount\s*:\s*u\d+", src)) or bool(re.search(r"amount\s*:\s*u64", src))
    status = "PASS" if (has_student_field and has_amount_field) else "FAIL"
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_GRANT_FIELDS",
        title="Grant has required fields (student, amount)",
        status=status,
        details=f"student field={'YES' if has_student_field else 'NO'}, amount field={'YES' if has_amount_field else 'NO'}"
    ))

    # 2) AdminCap exists
    has_admincap = bool(re.search(r"struct\s+AdminCap\b", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_ADMINCAP_STRUCT",
        title="AdminCap struct exists",
        status="PASS" if has_admincap else "FAIL",
        details="Found `struct AdminCap`." if has_admincap else "Missing `struct AdminCap`."
    ))

    # 3) mint signature requires AdminCap (by reference or value)
    # examples: fun mint(student: address, amount: u64, cap: &AdminCap, ctx: &mut TxContext)
    mint_sig = re.search(r"fun\s+mint\s*\(([^)]*)\)", src)
    if mint_sig:
        sig = mint_sig.group(1)
        uses_admincap_param = "AdminCap" in sig
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="MOVE_MINT_CAPABILITY",
            title="mint requires AdminCap capability parameter",
            status="PASS" if uses_admincap_param else "FAIL",
            details=f"mint signature: {sig.strip()}"
        ))
    else:
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="MOVE_MINT_CAPABILITY",
            title="mint requires AdminCap capability parameter",
            status="FAIL",
            details="Could not locate `fun mint(...)`."
        ))

    # 4) mint emits event
    emits_event = bool(re.search(r"event::emit", src)) or bool(re.search(r"emit", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_EVENT_EMIT",
        title="mint emits an event",
        status="PASS" if emits_event else "WARN",
        details="Found event emission pattern." if emits_event else "No clear event emission pattern found (heuristic)."
    ))

    # 5) mint transfers Grant to student
    transfers = bool(re.search(r"transfer::public_transfer", src)) or bool(re.search(r"public_transfer", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_TRANSFER",
        title="mint transfers Grant to student",
        status="PASS" if transfers else "FAIL",
        details="Found `transfer::public_transfer` usage." if transfers else "No `transfer::public_transfer` found."
    ))

    # 6) basic sanity: no abort (you removed misuse earlier)
    has_abort = bool(re.search(r"\babort\b", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_ABORT_USAGE",
        title="No abort misuse in module",
        status="PASS" if not has_abort else "WARN",
        details="No abort found." if not has_abort else "Found `abort` keyword; verify it is intended."
    ))

    return checks

def _scan_evidence(student_addr: Optional[str]) -> List[CheckResult]:
    checks: List[CheckResult] = []

    # Evidence presence
    for k, p in ARTIFACTS.items():
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id=f"EVIDENCE_FILE_{k.upper()}",
            title=f"Evidence file exists: {p.name}",
            status="PASS" if _exists(p) else "FAIL",
            details=str(p)
        ))

    mint_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["mint_output"])
    # look for a digest-ish base58/equals patterns or "Transaction Digest"
    has_digest = bool(re.search(r"\bDigest\b|\btransaction\b|\b[1-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-z]{20,}\b", mint_txt, re.IGNORECASE))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_MINT_HAS_DIGEST",
        title="Mint output contains transaction/digest signal",
        status="PASS" if has_digest else "WARN",
        details="Found digest/tx hint." if has_digest else "No obvious digest found (format may differ)."
    ))

    objects_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["objects_student"])
    has_grant_type = "grant::Grant" in objects_txt
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_OBJECTS_SHOW_GRANT",
        title="Student objects output shows grant::Grant",
        status="PASS" if has_grant_type else "FAIL",
        details="Found grant::Grant in objects listing." if has_grant_type else "grant::Grant not found in objects listing."
    ))

    grant_obj_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["grant_object"])
    # Ownership
    if student_addr:
        owned = student_addr.lower() in grant_obj_txt.lower()
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="EVIDENCE_GRANT_OWNER_MATCH",
            title="Grant object owner matches student address",
            status="PASS" if owned else "FAIL",
            details=f"Expected owner contains {student_addr}"
        ))
    else:
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="EVIDENCE_GRANT_OWNER_MATCH",
            title="Grant object owner matches student address",
            status="WARN",
            details="Student address not resolved from env.txt; cannot verify owner match programmatically."
        ))

    # Amount check
    exp_amount = EXPECTED["expected_amount"]
    has_amount = re.search(r"\bamount\b.*\b" + re.escape(exp_amount) + r"\b", grant_obj_txt)
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_GRANT_AMOUNT_MATCH",
        title=f"Grant object amount matches expected ({exp_amount})",
        status="PASS" if has_amount else "WARN",
        details="Found expected amount in grant-object evidence." if has_amount else "Could not confirm amount in evidence (format may differ)."
    ))

    test_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["test_output"])
    tests_pass = "Test result: OK" in test_txt or "[ PASS" in test_txt
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_TESTS_PASS",
        title="Move unit tests pass",
        status="PASS" if tests_pass else "FAIL",
        details="Found PASS/OK markers in test-output." if tests_pass else "No PASS/OK marker found in test-output."
    ))

    return checks

def _overall_status(checks: List[CheckResult]) -> str:
    if any(c.status == "FAIL" for c in checks):
        return "FAIL"
    if any(c.status == "WARN" for c in checks):
        return "WARN"
    return "PASS"

def main() -> int:
    student_addr = _load_student_addr_from_env()
    if student_addr:
        EXPECTED["student_addr"] = student_addr

    move_src_txt = _read_text(MOVE_SRC)
    move_checks = _scan_move_structure(move_src_txt) if move_src_txt else [CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_SRC_EXISTS",
        title="grant.move exists and readable",
        status="FAIL",
        details=f"Could not read {MOVE_SRC}"
    )]

    evidence_checks = _scan_evidence(student_addr)

    all_checks = move_checks + evidence_checks
    status = _overall_status(all_checks)

    now = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
    report: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "schema_version": "1.0",
        "run_utc": now,
        "scope": "WS01_Move_Foundations",
        "module": "scholarflow_core",
        "status": status,
        "inputs": {
            "move_source": str(MOVE_SRC),
            "artifacts": {k: str(v) for k, v in ARTIFACTS.items()},
            "student_addr": student_addr,
            "expected_amount": EXPECTED["expected_amount"],
        },
        "checks": [asdict(c) for c in all_checks],
        "summary": {
            "pass": sum(1 for c in all_checks if c.status == "PASS"),
            "warn": sum(1 for c in all_checks if c.status == "WARN"),
            "fail": sum(1 for c in all_checks if c.status == "FAIL"),
        },
    }

    out_dir = ROOT / "qa" / "aiqa" / "reports"
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    json_path = out_dir / "ws01_aiqa_report.json"
    md_path = out_dir / "ws01_aiqa_summary.md"

    json_path.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

    # markdown summary
    lines = []
    lines.append(f"# WS01 AI-QA Summary\n")
    lines.append(f"- Run (UTC): {now}\n")
    lines.append(f"- Status: **{status}**\n")
    if student_addr:
        lines.append(f"- Student address: `{student_addr}`\n")
    lines.append(f"- Expected amount: `{EXPECTED['expected_amount']}`\n")
    lines.append("\n## Checks\n")
    for c in all_checks:
        lines.append(f"- **{c.status}** `{c.id}` — {c.title}\n  - {c.details}\n")
    lines.append("\n## Totals\n")
    lines.append(f"- PASS: {report['summary']['pass']}\n")
    lines.append(f"- WARN: {report['summary']['warn']}\n")
    lines.append(f"- FAIL: {report['summary']['fail']}\n")

    md_path.write_text("".join(lines), encoding="utf-8")

    # Exit codes: PASS/WARN => 0 (for workshop). FAIL => 2
    return 2 if status == "FAIL" else 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

## 4) Understand WS01 Move code (working-state reading map)

This is how to read `grant.move` like an engineer:

### A) Data model
- `struct Grant has key { id: UID, student: address, amount: u64 }`
  - `has key` means it’s an on-chain object
  - `UID` gives object identity
  - `student` is recipient address
  - `amount` is your minted value (87)

- `struct AdminCap has key { id: UID }`
  - capability object; possession == authority

### B) init
- `fun init(ctx: &mut TxContext)` typically:
  - creates AdminCap
  - transfers AdminCap to publisher (your address)

### C) mint
- `fun mint(student, amount, cap, ctx)`:
  - requires `AdminCap` parameter to enforce authorization
  - creates `Grant` object
  - emits event (so indexers can track)
  - transfers grant to student address

### D) What your evidence already proved
- `objects-student.txt` shows `grant::Grant` exists
- `grant-object.txt` shows:
  - owner = your student address
  - amount = 87

So mint is working in real chain state.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
WS01 AI-QA (programmatic review) - minimal but enterprise-shaped.

- Scans Move source for required structural patterns (lightweight "AST-ish" heuristics).
- Validates evidence artifacts produced by manual tests.
- Emits:
  - JSON report (machine-readable)
  - Markdown summary (human-readable)
- Exit code:
  0 = PASS
  2 = FAIL
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, asdict
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]  # .../WS01_Move_Foundations
MOVE_SRC = ROOT / "move" / "scholarflow_core" / "sources" / "grant.move"

ARTIFACTS = {
    "mint_output": ROOT / "artifacts" / "mint-output.txt",
    "objects_student": ROOT / "artifacts" / "objects-student.txt",
    "grant_object": ROOT / "artifacts" / "grant-object.txt",
    "test_output": ROOT / "artifacts" / "test-output.txt",
}

EXPECTED = {
    "student_addr": None,   # optional: filled from env.txt if present
    "expected_amount": "87" # you minted 87 (from your logs)
}

ENV_TXT = ROOT / "artifacts" / "env.txt"

@dataclass
class CheckResult:
    id: str
    title: str
    status: str  # PASS/FAIL/WARN
    details: str

def _read_text(p: Path) -> str:
    try:
        return p.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return ""
    except Exception as e:
        return f"<<ERROR reading {p}: {e}>>"

def _exists(p: Path) -> bool:
    return p.exists() and p.is_file()

def _load_student_addr_from_env() -> Optional[str]:
    if not _exists(ENV_TXT):
        return None
    txt = _read_text(ENV_TXT)
    # best-effort: match 0x... length 66 typical
    m = re.search(r"(0x[a-fA-F0-9]{64})", txt)
    return m.group(1) if m else None

def _scan_move_structure(src: str) -> List[CheckResult]:
    checks: List[CheckResult] = []

    # 1) Grant struct contains key + fields student + amount
    has_grant_struct = bool(re.search(r"struct\s+Grant\s+has\s+key", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_GRANT_KEY",
        title="Grant is a key object",
        status="PASS" if has_grant_struct else "FAIL",
        details="Found `struct Grant has key`." if has_grant_struct else "Missing `struct Grant has key`."
    ))

    has_student_field = bool(re.search(r"student\s*:\s*address", src))
    has_amount_field = bool(re.search(r"amount\s*:\s*u\d+", src)) or bool(re.search(r"amount\s*:\s*u64", src))
    status = "PASS" if (has_student_field and has_amount_field) else "FAIL"
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_GRANT_FIELDS",
        title="Grant has required fields (student, amount)",
        status=status,
        details=f"student field={'YES' if has_student_field else 'NO'}, amount field={'YES' if has_amount_field else 'NO'}"
    ))

    # 2) AdminCap exists
    has_admincap = bool(re.search(r"struct\s+AdminCap\b", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_ADMINCAP_STRUCT",
        title="AdminCap struct exists",
        status="PASS" if has_admincap else "FAIL",
        details="Found `struct AdminCap`." if has_admincap else "Missing `struct AdminCap`."
    ))

    # 3) mint signature requires AdminCap (by reference or value)
    # examples: fun mint(student: address, amount: u64, cap: &AdminCap, ctx: &mut TxContext)
    mint_sig = re.search(r"fun\s+mint\s*\(([^)]*)\)", src)
    if mint_sig:
        sig = mint_sig.group(1)
        uses_admincap_param = "AdminCap" in sig
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="MOVE_MINT_CAPABILITY",
            title="mint requires AdminCap capability parameter",
            status="PASS" if uses_admincap_param else "FAIL",
            details=f"mint signature: {sig.strip()}"
        ))
    else:
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="MOVE_MINT_CAPABILITY",
            title="mint requires AdminCap capability parameter",
            status="FAIL",
            details="Could not locate `fun mint(...)`."
        ))

    # 4) mint emits event
    emits_event = bool(re.search(r"event::emit", src)) or bool(re.search(r"emit", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_EVENT_EMIT",
        title="mint emits an event",
        status="PASS" if emits_event else "WARN",
        details="Found event emission pattern." if emits_event else "No clear event emission pattern found (heuristic)."
    ))

    # 5) mint transfers Grant to student
    transfers = bool(re.search(r"transfer::public_transfer", src)) or bool(re.search(r"public_transfer", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_TRANSFER",
        title="mint transfers Grant to student",
        status="PASS" if transfers else "FAIL",
        details="Found `transfer::public_transfer` usage." if transfers else "No `transfer::public_transfer` found."
    ))

    # 6) basic sanity: no abort (you removed misuse earlier)
    has_abort = bool(re.search(r"\babort\b", src))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_ABORT_USAGE",
        title="No abort misuse in module",
        status="PASS" if not has_abort else "WARN",
        details="No abort found." if not has_abort else "Found `abort` keyword; verify it is intended."
    ))

    return checks

def _scan_evidence(student_addr: Optional[str]) -> List[CheckResult]:
    checks: List[CheckResult] = []

    # Evidence presence
    for k, p in ARTIFACTS.items():
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id=f"EVIDENCE_FILE_{k.upper()}",
            title=f"Evidence file exists: {p.name}",
            status="PASS" if _exists(p) else "FAIL",
            details=str(p)
        ))

    mint_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["mint_output"])
    # look for a digest-ish base58/equals patterns or "Transaction Digest"
    has_digest = bool(re.search(r"\bDigest\b|\btransaction\b|\b[1-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-z]{20,}\b", mint_txt, re.IGNORECASE))
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_MINT_HAS_DIGEST",
        title="Mint output contains transaction/digest signal",
        status="PASS" if has_digest else "WARN",
        details="Found digest/tx hint." if has_digest else "No obvious digest found (format may differ)."
    ))

    objects_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["objects_student"])
    has_grant_type = "grant::Grant" in objects_txt
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_OBJECTS_SHOW_GRANT",
        title="Student objects output shows grant::Grant",
        status="PASS" if has_grant_type else "FAIL",
        details="Found grant::Grant in objects listing." if has_grant_type else "grant::Grant not found in objects listing."
    ))

    grant_obj_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["grant_object"])
    # Ownership
    if student_addr:
        owned = student_addr.lower() in grant_obj_txt.lower()
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="EVIDENCE_GRANT_OWNER_MATCH",
            title="Grant object owner matches student address",
            status="PASS" if owned else "FAIL",
            details=f"Expected owner contains {student_addr}"
        ))
    else:
        checks.append(CheckResult(
            id="EVIDENCE_GRANT_OWNER_MATCH",
            title="Grant object owner matches student address",
            status="WARN",
            details="Student address not resolved from env.txt; cannot verify owner match programmatically."
        ))

    # Amount check
    exp_amount = EXPECTED["expected_amount"]
    has_amount = re.search(r"\bamount\b.*\b" + re.escape(exp_amount) + r"\b", grant_obj_txt)
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_GRANT_AMOUNT_MATCH",
        title=f"Grant object amount matches expected ({exp_amount})",
        status="PASS" if has_amount else "WARN",
        details="Found expected amount in grant-object evidence." if has_amount else "Could not confirm amount in evidence (format may differ)."
    ))

    test_txt = _read_text(ARTIFACTS["test_output"])
    tests_pass = "Test result: OK" in test_txt or "[ PASS" in test_txt
    checks.append(CheckResult(
        id="EVIDENCE_TESTS_PASS",
        title="Move unit tests pass",
        status="PASS" if tests_pass else "FAIL",
        details="Found PASS/OK markers in test-output." if tests_pass else "No PASS/OK marker found in test-output."
    ))

    return checks

def _overall_status(checks: List[CheckResult]) -> str:
    if any(c.status == "FAIL" for c in checks):
        return "FAIL"
    if any(c.status == "WARN" for c in checks):
        return "WARN"
    return "PASS"

def main() -> int:
    student_addr = _load_student_addr_from_env()
    if student_addr:
        EXPECTED["student_addr"] = student_addr

    move_src_txt = _read_text(MOVE_SRC)
    move_checks = _scan_move_structure(move_src_txt) if move_src_txt else [CheckResult(
        id="MOVE_SRC_EXISTS",
        title="grant.move exists and readable",
        status="FAIL",
        details=f"Could not read {MOVE_SRC}"
    )]

    evidence_checks = _scan_evidence(student_addr)

    all_checks = move_checks + evidence_checks
    status = _overall_status(all_checks)

    now = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
    report: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "schema_version": "1.0",
        "run_utc": now,
        "scope": "WS01_Move_Foundations",
        "module": "scholarflow_core",
        "status": status,
        "inputs": {
            "move_source": str(MOVE_SRC),
            "artifacts": {k: str(v) for k, v in ARTIFACTS.items()},
            "student_addr": student_addr,
            "expected_amount": EXPECTED["expected_amount"],
        },
        "checks": [asdict(c) for c in all_checks],
        "summary": {
            "pass": sum(1 for c in all_checks if c.status == "PASS"),
            "warn": sum(1 for c in all_checks if c.status == "WARN"),
            "fail": sum(1 for c in all_checks if c.status == "FAIL"),
        },
    }

    out_dir = ROOT / "qa" / "aiqa" / "reports"
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    json_path = out_dir / "ws01_aiqa_report.json"
    md_path = out_dir / "ws01_aiqa_summary.md"

    json_path.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

    # markdown summary
    lines = []
    lines.append(f"# WS01 AI-QA Summary\n")
    lines.append(f"- Run (UTC): {now}\n")
    lines.append(f"- Status: **{status}**\n")
    if student_addr:
        lines.append(f"- Student address: `{student_addr}`\n")
    lines.append(f"- Expected amount: `{EXPECTED['expected_amount']}`\n")
    lines.append("\n## Checks\n")
    for c in all_checks:
        lines.append(f"- **{c.status}** `{c.id}` â€” {c.title}\n  - {c.details}\n")
    lines.append("\n## Totals\n")
    lines.append(f"- PASS: {report['summary']['pass']}\n")
    lines.append(f"- WARN: {report['summary']['warn']}\n")
    lines.append(f"- FAIL: {report['summary']['fail']}\n")

    md_path.write_text("".join(lines), encoding="utf-8")

    # Exit codes: PASS/WARN => 0 (for workshop). FAIL => 2
    return 2 if status == "FAIL" else 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

### 0213 · CHATGPT · 2026-02-22T15:34:10Z

22/02/2026 15:33 — Perfect run, Senthil. You hit **2 real issues** (both are good training moments):

1) **PS1 path bug** → it tried to run `...\qa\run_aiqa.py` instead of `...\qa\aiqa\run_aiqa.py`  
2) Your old `WS01-ai-review.md` is **not tracked by git**, so `git mv` failed. You moved it in Windows explorer/file system, so git sees it as **new untracked**.

Also: your dev-review file content has the **UTF-8 / encoding issue** (`â€“`, `â†’`, `âœ…`). We should fix that too (one-time cleanup).

Let’s fix everything cleanly, with exact commands.

---

## 1) Fix the AI-QA runner path (PS1)

Your PS1 currently does:

- `$root = Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot`  
That makes `$root = ...\qa\aiqa` **parent** → `...\qa` (WRONG)

You want `$root = $PSScriptRoot` (aiqa folder itself).

Replace your `run_aiqa.ps1` with this:

```powershell
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$aiqa = $PSScriptRoot                         # ...\WS01_Move_Foundations\qa\aiqa
$ws01 = Split-Path -Parent (Split-Path -Parent $aiqa)  # ...\WS01_Move_Foundations

Write-Host "WS01 AI-QA starting..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host "WS01 Root: $ws01"
Write-Host "AIQA Dir:  $aiqa"

python "$aiqa\run_aiqa.py"
$exit = $LASTEXITCODE

Write-Host "AI-QA exit code: $exit"
if ($exit -ne 0) {
  Write-Host "AI-QA FAIL (see reports folder)" -ForegroundColor Red
  exit $exit
}

Write-Host "AI-QA PASS/WARN (see reports folder)" -ForegroundColor Green
```

Now rerun:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\workshop\WS01_Move_Foundations\qa\aiqa\run_aiqa.ps1
```

After this, `reports/` should contain:
- `ws01_aiqa_report.json`
- `ws01_aiqa_summary.md`

### Commit the PS1 fix
```powershell
git add workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/qa/aiqa/run_aiqa.ps1
git commit -m "WS01: fix AI-QA runner path (run_aiqa.ps1)"
git tag WS01_AIQA_RUNNER_FIXED
```

---

## 2) Why your `git mv` failed + how to fix properly

### What happened
`git mv` failed because **WS01-ai-review.md was never committed/tracked**.  
Your `git status` earlier already showed it as **untracked**.

So you cannot `git mv` an untracked file. You must either:

### Option A (cleanest): add it directly in new location + delete old folder
You already moved the file into `docs/dev-review/WS01-dev-review.md`. Great.

Now just remove the empty folder (if it still exists) and add + commit:

```powershell
# remove old empty folder if it exists
if (Test-Path workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/docs/ai-review) {
  rmdir workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/docs/ai-review -Force -Recurse
}

git add workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/docs/dev-review/WS01-dev-review.md
git commit -m "WS01: add dev-review log (renamed from ai-review)"
git tag WS01_DEV_REVIEW_STABLE
```

That’s it. No `git mv` needed.

---

## 3) Is the dev-review content “correct”?
**Structurally yes** (it captures: mint flow, AdminCap gate, init transfer, ownership, tests).  
But we should **rename the title** inside the file because it still says “AI Review Log”. It should say:

- `# WS01 — Dev Review Log`

And we should fix the encoding so the symbols render correctly.

### Fix content + encoding (UTF-8)
Open in VS Code (recommended) and “Save with Encoding: UTF-8”.

Or do it via PowerShell quick replace (fast + safe):

```powershell
$path = ".\workshop\WS01_Move_Foundations\docs\dev-review\WS01-dev-review.md"
$content = Get-Content $path -Raw

# fix title
$content = $content -replace "WS01 â€“ AI Review Log", "WS01 — Dev Review Log"

# fix common mojibake sequences (best-effort)
$content = $content -replace "â€“", "—"
$content = $content -replace "â†’", "→"
$content = $content -replace "âœ…", "✅"
$content = $content -replace "âš ï¸", "⚠️"

# write UTF-8
[System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($path, $content, (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)))

git add $path
git commit -m "WS01: fix dev-review title + UTF-8 symbols"
git tag WS01_DEV_REVIEW_UTF8_FIXED
```

(That `$false` ensures “UTF-8 without BOM”, clean.)

---

## 4) Why reports folder is empty right now
Because the script **never ran successfully** (python couldn’t open the file), so it never wrote outputs.

Once you fix PS1 and rerun, you’ll see the reports.

---

## 5) One more important discipline: don’t tag “STABLE” before it runs
You already tagged `WS01_AIQA_STABLE` **before** it produced reports. Not fatal, but for discipline:

- leave that tag as historical
- create a new tag after reports exist, like:
  - `WS01_AIQA_REPORTS_STABLE`

After you rerun successfully:

```powershell
git add workshop/WS01_Move_Foundations/qa/aiqa/reports/
git commit -m "WS01: add AI-QA generated reports (json + md)"
git tag WS01_AIQA_REPORTS_STABLE
```

---

## 6) The training “magic” (what you learn from this)
This is the exact enterprise lesson:

- **Code exists** ≠ **Gate exists**
- **Gate exists** ≠ **Gate runs**
- **Gate runs** ≠ **Gate has evidence outputs**
- **Evidence exists** ≠ **Evidence is version-controlled**
- **Version-controlled** ≠ **Correctly encoded / readable**

You just lived all 5 layers in 10 minutes. That’s real QA maturity.
